# A uniform Hilbert transform estimate for Lipschitz directions

Coordinate `EN-110-000-0003-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-TO-H-0186` | `PM-083.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `A-uniform-Hilbert-transform-estimate-for-Lipschitz-directions-September-25-2026` | date 2026-09-25 | derived from the openai/math preprint A-uniform-Hilbert-transform-estimate-for-Lipschitz-directions-September-25-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove a uniform <i>L</i><sup>2</sup> bound for the Hilbert transform along a Lipschitz unit vector field in the plane, with integration restricted to a fixed absolute multiple of the reciprocal Lipschitz seminorm. The bound is uniform in the inner truncation and holds for fields depending on both coordinates. This gives an affirmative answer to Stein's weak-$`(2,2)`$ conjecture.",
  "canonical_anchor": "pm-s7",
  "claim_status": "CORPUS_CLAIMED",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a uniform <i>L</i><sup>2</sup> bound for the Hilbert transform along a Lipschitz unit vector field in the plane, with integration restricted to a fixed absolute multiple of the reciprocal Lipschitz seminorm. The bound is uniform in the inner truncation and holds for fields depending on both coordinates. This gives an affirmative answer to Stein's weak-$`(2,2)`$ conjecture.

- **claim-main-uniform-l2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are absolute constants $a_*\in(0,1/2)$ and $C_*<\infty$ such that every Lipschitz field $v:\mathbb R^2\to S^1$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(v)\le1$ satisfies $$\sup_{0<\epsilon<a_*} \left\\|H_{v,a_*}^{\epsilon}f\right\\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^2)} \le C_*\left\\|f\right\\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^2)} \qquad(f\in\mathcal S(\mathbb R^2)).$$ Its symmetric principal value therefore satisfies $$\left\\|H_{v,a_*}f\right\\|_2\le C_*\left\\|f\right\\|_2, \qquad \big\|\{x:\|H_{v,a_*}f(x)\|>\lambda\}\big\| \le C_*^2\lambda^{-2}\left\\|f\right\\|_2^2 \quad(\lambda>0).$$
  - Explanation: The short directional Hilbert transform along a Lipschitz unit field in the plane has an L2 bound that is uniform in the inner truncation. This gives the weak-type conclusion and answers Stein's question.
- **claim-lacey-li-kakeya** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-kakeya)): Let $v:\mathbb R^2\to S^1$ be Lipschitz and $0<\delta<1$. Restrict to rectangles satisfying $$L(R)\le \frac1{100\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(v)},\qquad \left\|R\cap v^{-1}(\operatorname{EX}(R))\right\|\ge\delta\left\|R\right\|,$$ where the first restriction is void when $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(v)=0$. Then $$\mathcal M_{v,\delta}h(z) =\sup_R\frac{\mathbf 1_R(z)}{\left\|R\right\|}\int_R\left\|h\right\| \quad\text{satisfies}\quad \left\\|\mathcal M_{v,\delta}h\right\\|_{L^{2,\infty}} \le C\delta^{-1/2}\left\\|h\right\\|_2.$$ Here the supremum is tested at every point of $R$, and the constant is independent of $v$ and $\delta$.
  - Explanation: A maximal operator over short rectangles on which the field has a popular direction has a weak L2 bound whose cost is a negative power of the popularity.
- **claim-fixed-time-lp-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-red-fixed-time)): For $\left\|t\right\|\,C_{\rm Lip}<1$, define $$A_t h(x,y)=h(x-t,y-tu(x,y)).$$ For $1\le p<\infty$, $$\label{red:translate-bound} \left\\|A_t h\right\\|_p \le (1-\left\|t\right\|\,C_{\rm Lip})^{-1/p}\left\\|h\right\\|_p,$$ and $A_t$ is a contraction on $L^\infty$. Consequently, replacing either symmetric endpoint at scale $r$ by a measurable endpoint between $c r$ and $C r$, with $c,C>0$ fixed and $\max(1,C)r C_{\rm Lip}<1/2$, costs a bounded $L^p$ operator, independently of $r$.
  - Explanation: The sheared translation along a Lipschitz slope field is bounded on Lp, so moving truncation endpoints costs a bounded operator independent of scale.
- **claim-band-restricted** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-band-restricted)): For the fixed absolute constants in <a href="#red:local-operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:local-operator">[red:local-operator]</a>, there is an absolute $C$ such that, for every field $u$ with $\left\|u\right\|\le1$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(u)\le C_{\rm Lip}$, every $0<\epsilon<a$, every dyadic $w$, and every vertical band cutoff $P_w$, $$\label{eq:src-1} \left\|\langle H_{u,a}^{\epsilon}P_w f,g\rangle\right\| \le C\bigl(1+\left\|\log(\|F\|/\|E\|)\right\|\bigr)^{-3} \sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|}$$ whenever $F,E\subset I\times\mathbb R$ have positive finite measure, $\left\|f\right\|\le\mathbf 1_F$, $\left\|g\right\|\le\mathbf 1_E$, and $\left\|I\right\|\le b_0$.
  - Explanation: On one vertical frequency band the restricted bilinear form gains a logarithmic factor in the measure ratio, uniformly in the band scale and inner truncation.
- **claim-restricted-to-strong** (bound, [source](#stmt-red-restricted-strong)): Suppose a bilinear form $\mathcal B$ satisfies $$\left\|\mathcal B(f,g)\right\| \le C_0(1+\|\log(\|F\|/\|E\|)\|)^{-3}\sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|} \quad (\|f\|\le\mathbf 1_F,\ \|g\|\le\mathbf 1_E)$$ on finite-measure sets in a fixed measurable domain. Then $$\left\|\mathcal B(f,g)\right\|\le C C_0\left\\|f\right\\|_2\left\\|g\right\\|_2$$ for finite amplitude decompositions, and hence for all $L^2$ inputs whenever the form is obtained by an $L^2$-continuous truncation or by completion from those inputs.
  - Explanation: A restricted-type bound with a cubic logarithmic decay in the measure ratio sums over amplitude levels to a strong L2 bilinear bound.
- **claim-carleson-embedding** (bound, [source](#stmt-red-carleson)): For every fixed integer $c$, every $q\in L^\infty(\mathbb R)$, and every $h\in L^2(\mathbb R)$, $$\label{red:carleson-estimate} \sum_k\left\\|(\Delta_kq)S_{k+c}h\right\\|_2^2 \le C_c\left\\|q\right\\|_\infty^2\left\\|h\right\\|_2^2.$$
  - Explanation: A Littlewood-Paley Carleson embedding controls the quadratic sum of high-frequency pieces of a bounded function times low-frequency pieces of an L2 function.
- **claim-lipschitz-commutator** (bound, [source](#stmt-red-commutator)): Let $b\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb R)$, and let $T=\mathfrak m(D)$, where $\mathfrak m$ is smooth away from zero and $$A(\mathfrak m) =\max_{0\le r\le4}\sup_{\zeta\ne0} \|\zeta\|^r\|\mathfrak m^{(r)}(\zeta)\|<\infty.$$ Then $$\label{red:commutator-estimate} \left\\|[b,T]\partial h\right\\|_2 \le C A(\mathfrak m)\left\\|b'\right\\|_\infty\left\\|h\right\\|_2.$$ The commutator is initially defined on the Sobolev space $W^{1,2}(\mathbb R)$; the estimate gives its bounded extension to $L^2$. Its constant is independent of $\left\\|b\right\\|_\infty$ and of all frequency truncations.
  - Explanation: The commutator of a Lipschitz multiplication with a smooth Fourier multiplier composed with a derivative is L2 bounded by the Lipschitz seminorm, independently of the sup norm.
- **claim-composition-commutator** (bound, [source](#stmt-red-composition-commutator)): For $\left\|t\right\|\le b_0$ and $T$ as in [red:commutator](#red:commutator), $$\label{red:composition-bound} \left\\|TC_t h-C_tTh\right\\|_2 \le C A(\mathfrak m)\left\|t\right\|\,\left\\|h\right\\|_2.$$ The constant is uniform over $\left\|u\right\|\le1$, $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(u)\le C_{\rm Lip}$.
  - Explanation: Commuting a vertical multiplier with the vertical shear composition costs a factor proportional to the time parameter, which cancels the singular measure.
- **claim-band-implies-main** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-band-implies-main)): The uniform restricted estimate in [thm:band-restricted](#thm:band-restricted) implies [thm:main](#thm:main).
  - Explanation: The uniform restricted band estimate implies the main theorem by square-function reassembly across bands and localization.
- **claim-time-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-red-time-reduction)): For any fixed $1<p<\infty$, the contribution of $\max(\epsilon,\tau)<\|t\|<a$ to $HP_w$ has $L^p$ norm at most $C_p(1+L)$ if $L\ge L_0$, and at most a fixed $C_p$ otherwise. On the remaining times below $w$, replacing $u$ by zero costs a uniformly bounded $L^p$ operator. The resulting horizontal symmetric truncation is uniformly bounded on $L^p$.
  - Explanation: Long times cost at most a logarithmic factor in L and very short times can be replaced by a horizontal truncation at bounded Lp cost.
- **claim-scale-truncation** (bound, [source](#stmt-red-scale-truncation)): For dyadic $0<r\le R$, $$\label{red:scale-kernel-error} \int_\mathbb R\left\| \frac{\mathbf 1_{\{r<\|t\|<R\}}}{t} -\sum_{r\le\ell\le R}k_\ell(t) \right\|\,\mathrm dt\le C.$$ Also, $$\label{red:scale-first-moment} \int_\mathbb R\|t\|\left\|\sum_{r\le\ell\le R}k_\ell(t)\right\| \,\mathrm dt\le C R.$$ Both constants are independent of the number of scales. Changing either spatial endpoint by a fixed factor preserves the first bound.
  - Explanation: A sharp truncation of the kernel is approximated by a finite sum of smooth dyadic kernels with errors and first moment independent of the number of scales.
- **claim-bin-levels** (bound, [source](#stmt-red-bin-levels)): Let $h:\mathbb R^2\to\mathcal H$ satisfy $$\left\\|h\right\\|_\infty\le Q,\qquad \left\\|h\right\\|_1\le Q M_0,\qquad \left\\|h\right\\|_2^2\le C_0M_0,$$ where $M_0>0$ and $Q\le C\exp(C L^{0.9})$. Write $$A_i=\{2^{i-1}<\left\\|h(\cdot)\right\\|_{\mathcal H}\le2^i\}, \qquad a_i=2^i\sqrt{\|A_i\|/M_0}.$$ For sufficiently large absolute $L$, $$\label{red:level-weight-bounds} \sum_i a_i\le C\sqrt{1+L},\qquad \sum_{\{i:\,\|\log(\|A_i\|/M_0)\|>L/4\}}a_i \le C e^{-cL}.$$ Empty level sets are omitted. The constants depend on the fixed constants in the hypotheses, not on the dimension of $\mathcal H$.
  - Explanation: After bin projection the normalized amplitude-level weights sum with only a square-root-logarithmic loss and have exponentially small tails, independently of the Hilbert space dimension.
- **claim-packet-representation** (construction, [source](#stmt-red-packet-representation)): The diagonal form $$\sum_j\langle\mathcal A_{u,\mathcal L} \widetilde Q_j f_j,g_j\rangle$$ is a sum, with absolutely summable scalar coefficients, of averages of forms <a href="#eq:src-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-3">[eq:src-3]</a>. Each form satisfies [red:packets](#red:packets), uniformly in $w,m,\mathcal L$, and has one common angular grid and common spatial lattices across components. The same assertion holds for $m=1$ with the original positive-frequency band cutoff in place of $\widetilde Q_1$. The sums can be obtained as limits of finite packet collections.
  - Explanation: The diagonalized multiplier form is an absolutely summable average of packet forms with common lattices and one common angular grid.
- **claim-model-suffices** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-model-suffices)): Suppose that the following bounds hold, uniformly for every admissible finite form $\Lambda_{\mathcal S}$ in [red:packets](#red:packets), with the fixed packet constants there. The field $u:\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb R$ is bounded and measurable, the sets $F,E\subset\mathbb R^2$ are measurable with positive finite measure, and the input and test satisfy $\left\\|f(z)\right\\|_{\mathcal H}\le\mathbf 1_F(z)$, $\left\\|g(z)\right\\|_{\mathcal H}\le\mathbf 1_E(z)$.* 1. *For $m\ge1$, there is a fixed exponent $C_1$ such that $$\label{red:model-basic} \|\Lambda_{\mathcal S}(f,g)\| \le C(1+\log(2m))^{C_1}\sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|}.$$* 2. *For $L\ge L_0$, with $\tau,m,W$ as in <a href="#red:small-time-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:small-time-parameters">[red:small-time-parameters]</a>, assume in addition that $\left\|u\right\|\le1$, $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(u)\le C_{\rm Lip}$, and $F,E\subset I\times\mathbb R$ for an interval $I$ with $\left\|I\right\|\le b_0$. Whenever $L/2\le\log\sqrt{\|E\|/\|F\|}\le2L$, $$\label{red:model-improved} \|\Lambda_{\mathcal S}(f,g)\| \le C L^{-20}\sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|}.$$* 3. *For $m=1$, one scalar component, and $\|E\|\le\|F\|$, there is a fixed $c>0$ such that $$\label{red:model-reverse} \|\Lambda_{\mathcal S}(f,g)\| \le C\sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|}(\|E\|/\|F\|)^c.$$* *Then holds. The constants remain uniform in $w$, $\epsilon$, and the number of retained scales.
  - Explanation: Three packet-form bounds (basic, improved at large positive disparity, reverse disparity) suffice for the band estimate, with the stated uniformity.
- **claim-ordinary-model-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-tile-prop-model)): Let the bin Hilbert space be $\mathcal H=\mathbb C^J$, where $J\leq C_{\rm bin}m$. If $\left\\|g(z)\right\\|_{\mathcal H}\leq\mathbf 1_E(z)$ and $D_E(s)\leq\gamma$ for every $s\in\mathcal S$, with $0<\gamma\leq C$, then $$\label{eq:src-4} \|\Lambda_{\mathcal S}(f,g)\| \leq C(1+\log(2m))^C\gamma^{1/4} \|E\|^{1/2}\left\\|f\right\\|_{L^2(\mathcal H)}.$$ In the scalar case, if $\|f\|\leq\mathbf 1_F$, $\|g\|\leq\mathbf 1_E$, and $0<\|E\|<\|F\|<\infty$, then $$\label{tile:eq-reverse-disparity} \|\Lambda_{\mathcal S}(f,g)\| \leq C\sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|} \left(\frac{\|E\|}{\|F\|}\right)^{9/20}.$$ Both conclusions hold uniformly for every subcollection of $\mathcal S$.
  - Explanation: Ordinary size and density selection give a density-power bound and a reverse-disparity power gain, uniformly over subcollections, with a logarithmic loss in the number of bins.
- **claim-separated-synthesis** (bound, [source](#stmt-tile-lem-synthesis)): Let $\mathcal T$ be separated tiles satisfying <a href="#tile:eq-tree" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tile:eq-tree">[tile:eq-tree]</a>, and let $\Upsilon_s$ be either of their packet families $\Phi_s$ or $\Psi_s$. For every fixed $J_0$, $$\begin{aligned} \left\\|\mathfrak p_{T,J_0} \sum_{s\in\mathcal T}c_s\Upsilon_s\right\\|_2 &\leq C_{J_0} \left(\sum_{s\in\mathcal T}\|c_s\|^2\|R_s\|\right)^{1/2}, \label{tile:eq-synthesis}\\ \left\\|\mathfrak p_{T,J_0} \sup_{a<b}\left\| \sum_{\substack{s\in\mathcal T\\a<\ell_s\leq b}} c_s\Upsilon_s\right\|\right\\|_2 &\leq C_{J_0} \left(\sum_{s\in\mathcal T}\|c_s\|^2\|R_s\|\right)^{1/2}. \label{tile:eq-maximal-synthesis} \end{aligned}$$ The same statements hold for Hilbert-valued sums $\bigl(\sum_s c_{s,j}\Upsilon_{s,j}\bigr)_j$, with $\sum_s\left\\|c_s\right\\|_{\mathcal H}^2\|R_s\|$ on the right and constants independent of $J$. Bounded scalar factors depending on $s,j$, such as $b_{s,j}(t)$, can be incorporated into the coefficients.
  - Explanation: Separated packets in a tree satisfy weighted L2 synthesis and maximal-synthesis bounds, also for Hilbert-valued sums with constants independent of the dimension.
- **claim-size-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-tile-lem-size)): Suppose $\operatorname{size}(v)\leq C_*\sigma$, with $C_*$ fixed. One can remove a collection of trees so that the remaining size is at most $\sigma$ and $$\label{tile:eq-size-budget} \sum_T\|R_T\|\leq C\sigma^{-2}\left\\|f\right\\|_2^2.$$ The removed tiles partition into ordinary trees satisfying <a href="#tile:eq-tree" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tile:eq-tree">[tile:eq-tree]</a>; their total top area has the same bound.
  - Explanation: Trees of large coefficient size can be removed with total top area controlled by the inverse square size times the L2 energy of the input.
- **claim-density-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-tile-lem-density)): For $0<\eta\leq C$, all tiles with $D_E(s)>\eta$ can be assigned to keeper boxes $R_T$, of dyadic length $l_T\leq\tau$, width $W$, and slope $t_T$, with the following properties. There are pairwise disjoint measurable sets $E_T$ such that $$\begin{gathered} E_T\subset E\cap K_\eta R_T \cap\{u\in\Omega(t_T,l_T)\},\qquad \|E_T\|\geq c\eta\|R_T\|, \label{tile:eq-density-witnesses}\\ \sum_T\|R_T\|\leq C\eta^{-1}\|E\|,\qquad K_\eta\leq C(1+\eta^{-1/100}), \label{tile:eq-keeper-budget}\\ \ell_s\leq l_T,\qquad t_T\in\Omega_s,\qquad z_s\in C K_\eta R_T \quad\text{for each tile assigned to }T. \label{tile:eq-density-assignment} \end{gathered}$$ After partitioning by centers, the assigned tiles form ordinary test trees of total top area at most $$\label{tile:eq-density-budget} C\eta^{-1}(1+\eta^{-1/20})\|E\|.$$
  - Explanation: Tiles of large density are assigned to keeper boxes with disjoint witness sets and total top area controlled by inverse density times the measure of the test set.
- **claim-tree-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-tile-lem-tree)): Suppose a scalar tree $\mathcal T$ satisfies $D_E(s)\leq\eta$ on its tiles and has size at most $\sigma$. If $\|g\|\leq\mathbf 1_E$, then $$\label{tile:eq-tree-bound} \|\Lambda_{\mathcal T}(f,g)\| \leq C\sigma\eta\|R_T\|.$$ The size assumption can be replaced by the same bound on any encompassing collection.
  - Explanation: A tree of bounded density and size contributes at most the product of size, density and top area.
- **claim-top-count-weak-l2** (bound, [source](#stmt-forest-prop-count)): For the tops in <a href="#forest:eq-witnesses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="forest:eq-witnesses">[forest:eq-witnesses]</a>, $$\label{eq:src-6} \left\\|n\right\\|_{L^{2,\infty}(\mathcal D)} \le C L^C\sqrt S.$$ More precisely, if $$D_*\ge\max(2K_0,\,2L^{C_{\mathrm{as}}},\,2),$$ then one may take the right-hand side to be $C L^{C_{\mathrm{as}}}D_*^3\sqrt S$, provided $D_*$ is chosen within an absolute factor of the displayed maximum. In particular, when $K_0=L^k$, a permissible exponent in <a href="#eq:src-6" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-6">[eq:src-6]</a> is $$\label{forest:eq-count-exponent} c_{\mathrm{cnt}}(k) =C_{\mathrm{as}}+3\max(k,C_{\mathrm{as}}).$$
  - Explanation: The weighted count of popular tops has a weak L2 bound controlled by the square root of the total top area, with a polynomial loss in L.
- **claim-mask-spectrum-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-forest-lem-mask)): The mask has Fourier support $$\label{forest:eq-mask-spectrum} \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\widehat q_T\subset \left\{(\xi,\eta): \|\xi+s_T\eta\|\le\frac{r_L}{L^A l_T},\quad \|\eta\|\le\frac{r_L}{L^A W}\right\}.$$ For sufficiently large $L$, with all other exponents fixed, $$\begin{aligned} \|q_T(z)\|&\le \exp(-L\log L) &&\text{if }\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_T(z)>K_0, \label{forest:eq-mask-outside}\\ \|q_T(z)\|h_T(z)^{-1}&\le C &&\text{for all }z, \label{forest:eq-mask-weight}\\ \|1-q_T(z)\|&\le C L^{1-A} &&\text{if }\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_T(z)\le L^{A/2}. \label{forest:eq-mask-inside} \end{aligned}$$ In particular multiplication by $q_T$ adds at most $O(L^{1-A})$ times the bandwidths of the top box to a Fourier support.
  - Explanation: The sinc-power mask has compact Fourier support, is superpolynomially small away from the significant region, and is close to one near the top.
- **claim-single-scale-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-conc-single-scale)): For a fixed scale $\ell=w/d$, and any subcollection of tiles satisfying <a href="#eq:src-9" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-9">[eq:src-9]</a> at this scale, $$\label{conc:original-positive} \sum_{\ell_s=\ell}\left\\|a_s\right\\|_{\mathcal H} \mathcal B_{s,n}^{t}(z)\leq C_n.$$ There are fixed constants $0<c<1<C'$ for which $$\begin{aligned} f_d(z)&=C\left( \mathbf 1_{\{cd\leq\left\|u(z)-t\right\|\leq C'B^4d\}} +\frac{\left\|u(z)-t\right\|}{d} \mathbf 1_{\{\left\|u(z)-t\right\|\leq cd\}}\right), \label{conc:error-input}\\ \sum_{\ell_s=\ell}\left\\|a_s^{\rm err}\right\\|_{\mathcal H} \mathcal B_{s,n}^{t}(z) &\leq C_n \big(k_{\ell,n'}*_{x} k_{W,n'}*_{Y_t}f_d\big)(z) \label{conc:error-majorant} \end{aligned}$$ for a fixed $n'>2$ with $2n'<n$. The constants are independent of the number of labels permitted in <a href="#eq:src-9" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-9">[eq:src-9]</a>.
  - Explanation: At a fixed scale the analyzing coefficients and their error parts are dominated by a bounded positive envelope, without dependence on the number of labels.
- **claim-coefficient-carleson** (bound, [source](#stmt-conc-coefficient-carleson)): For every $t$-box $Q$ of width $W$, the main coefficients satisfy $$\label{eq:src-10} \sum_{\substack{z_s\in Q\\\ell_s\leq\mathrm{length}(Q)}} \left\\|a_s^{\rm main}\right\\|_{\mathcal H}^2\left\|R_s\right\| \leq C\left\|Q\right\|.$$ The same estimate holds after restricting to any fixed subcollection.
  - Explanation: The main coefficients satisfy a Carleson packing condition on every box of the reference slope.
- **claim-annular-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-conc-annular-variation)): Suppose $F_k\in L^2(\mathbb R;\mathcal H)$ has Fourier support in $\{\nu:c_a2^k\leq\left\|\nu\right\|\leq C_a2^k\}$, with fixed positive $c_a,C_a$. Then $$\label{conc:annular-var-bound} \left\\|\mathcal V_3(F_k)\right\\|_{L^2(\mathbb R)} \leq C_{c_a,C_a} \left(\sum_k\left\\|F_k\right\\|_2^2\right)^{1/2}.$$ The constant is independent of the finite index range and of $\dim\mathcal H$.
  - Explanation: For annularly Fourier-supported pieces the interval-sum variation is bounded in L2 by the square sum of the pieces, independently of the dimension.
- **claim-john-nirenberg-local** (bound, [source](#stmt-conc-john-nirenberg)): Let $I$ be a finite interval and let $f$ be a locally integrable scalar function on $I$. Suppose that for each dyadic subinterval $J$ of $I$, $\left\|J\right\|^{-1}\int_J\left\|f-f_J\right\|\leq A$, where $f_J$ denotes its average. Then, for every $b\geq1$, $$\left(\frac1{\left\|I\right\|}\int_I\left\|f\right\|^b\right)^{1/b} \leq\left\|f_I\right\|+CbA.$$
  - Explanation: Normalized Lb norms on an interval are controlled by the mean plus the dyadic oscillation bound times the exponent.
- **claim-line-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-conc-line-moments)): For every slope $t$, every collection satisfying <a href="#eq:src-9" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-9">[eq:src-9]</a>, every line $Y_t=y_0$, every horizontal interval $I$ of length $l$ on that line, and every $b\geq2$, the normalized $L^b(I)$ norms of $\mathcal E_t$, $\mathcal S_t$, and $$\mathcal V_3\left(\sum_{\ell_s=2^k}\phi_s\right), \qquad \mathcal V_3\left(\sum_{\ell_s=2^k}q_{T(s)}\phi_s\right)$$ are at most $C b^C(1+\log B)^C$. The constants are uniform in the line, interval, finite collection, dimension, and number of scales. In particular the assertion holds for every fixed deterministic subcollection with the same constants.
  - Explanation: On every reference line the error, square function and variation sums have polynomial moment bounds, uniformly in the collection and for deterministic subcollections.
- **claim-top-good** (bound, [source](#stmt-conc-top-good)): For every fixed $J_*>0$ there are a fixed exponent $C_{\mathrm{top}}$ and a measurable set $\mathcal X_{\rm top}$ with $\left\|\mathcal X_{\rm top}\right\|\leq S e^{-J_*L}$ such that, for all tops $T$ and all $z\notin\mathcal X_{\rm top}$, $$\label{eq:src-11} \sup_{\mathcal J}\ \sup_{\left\|v\right\|\leq W} \left(\left\\|F_{T,\mathcal J}(z+(0,v))\right\\|_{\mathcal H} +\left\\|\mathcal D F_{T,\mathcal J}(z+(0,v))\right\\|_{\mathcal H}\right) \leq L^{C_{\mathrm{top}}}\big(h_T^{(1)}(z)\big)^8.$$ Independently of this exceptional set, for every $z$, every $l_*>0$, and every interval $\mathcal J\subset[l_*,\infty)$, $$\label{conc:top-horizontal-derivative} \left\\|(\partial_x+s_T\partial_y)F_{T,\mathcal J}(z)\right\\|_{\mathcal H} \leq \frac{L^{C_{\mathrm{top}}}}{l_*}\big(h_T^{(1)}(z)\big)^8.$$ All packet orders, $K_1$, and $C_{\mathrm{top}}$ can be fixed before $A$; they are independent of the later mass cutoff and hierarchy depth.
  - Explanation: Outside a small exceptional set every top sum and its derivative are polynomially bounded by a power of the top weight, in a vertical neighborhood.
- **claim-mask-removal** (bound, [source](#stmt-conc-mask-removal)): Given any fixed $H>0$, choose $A$ sufficiently large after $K_1$ and $C_{\mathrm{top}}$, and then choose a sufficiently large fixed count threshold exponent $C_n$. There is a measurable set $F_{\rm conc}\subset F$ such that $$\left\|F\setminus F_{\rm conc}\right\|\leq L^{-H}\left\|F\right\|, \qquad \int_{F_{\rm conc}}\left\\|P-P_q\right\\|_{\mathcal H} \leq L^{-H}N\left\|F\right\|, \qquad P_q=\sum_sq_{T(s)}\phi_s,$$ and <a href="#eq:src-11" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-11">[eq:src-11]</a> holds there for every top. On this set, $$\label{eq:src-12} n(z)=\sum_T h_T(z)\leq NL^{C_n}.$$ One has $K_0=L^{A+2}>K_1$, so both <a href="#eq:src-11" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-11">[eq:src-11]</a> and <a href="#conc:top-horizontal-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="conc:top-horizontal-derivative">[conc:top-horizontal-derivative]</a> also hold with $h_T$ in place of $h_T^{(1)}$.
  - Explanation: The original adjoint sum can be replaced by the masked sum on a large concentrated set, where the weighted top count is below a polynomial multiple of N.
- **claim-simultaneous-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-count-simultaneous)): There is a fixed exponent $C_D$ with the following property. For every fixed $J>0$ and all sufficiently large $L$, there is a measurable set $\mathcal E_{\mathrm{count}}\subset\mathbb R^2$ such that $$\label{count:exceptional-budget} \|\mathcal E_{\mathrm{count}}\|\leq S e^{-JL}.$$ Define $$\label{count:good-set} Z_0=F_{\mathrm{conc}}\setminus\mathcal E_{\mathrm{count}}, \qquad D=L^{C_D}.$$ For every $z\in Z_0$, every $\rho>0$, and every list $\mathcal L$ of tops significant at $z$ satisfying $$\label{count:list-hypotheses} \begin{split} &\max_{T\in\mathcal L}s_T-\min_{T\in\mathcal L}s_T\leq B\rho, \qquad d_T\leq\rho/B^2\quad(T\in\mathcal L),\\ &\|s_T-s_{T'}\|\geq\rho\quad(T,T'\in\mathcal L,\ T\ne T'), \end{split}$$ one has $\#\mathcal L\leq D/100$. The exponent $C_D$ depends only on the fixed geometric constants, including the already chosen mask exponent $A$. It is independent of the number of tops, the number of scales, and the bin Hilbert space.
  - Explanation: Outside a small exceptional set, every separated list of significant tops in the stated angular cluster and precision range has polynomially bounded size, simultaneously in the resolution.
- **claim-line-counting-lemma** (bound, [source](#stmt-count-line-lemma)): Let $(f_b)_{b\in\mathcal B}$ be a countable family of measurable functions on $\mathbb R$ such that $0\leq f_b\leq1$ and $\sum_b f_b\leq C_0$, where $C_0\geq1$. Fix $H>0$ and $0<\vartheta\leq1$. There is a measurable function $\mathcal N:\mathbb R\to[0,\infty]$ with the following properties. If $\mathcal B_x$ is any collection of distinct indices such that, for every $b\in\mathcal B_x$, some interval $I_b$ containing $x$ satisfies $$\label{count:line-average} 0<\|I_b\|\leq H,\qquad \|I_b\|^{-1}\int_{I_b}f_b\geq\vartheta,$$ then $\#\mathcal B_x\leq\mathcal N(x)$. Moreover, for every bounded interval $U$ and every $\lambda\geq0$, $$\label{count:line-tail} \|\{x\in U:\mathcal N(x)>\lambda\}\| \leq C(\|U\|+H)\exp(-c\vartheta\lambda/C_0),$$ where $C,c>0$ are absolute. If the $f_b$ depend jointly measurably on an additional parameter, $\mathcal N$ can be chosen jointly measurable in that parameter and $x$.
  - Explanation: A bounded family of functions with bounded sum has an exponentially distributed count of indices having a large average on some interval through a point.
- **claim-finite-fourier-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-phase-finite-fourier)): Let $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_d\in\mathbb R$, where $d\le D$, satisfy $\left\|\lambda_i-\lambda_j\right\|\ge\Delta>0$ for $i\ne j$. Let $\nu$ be any positive probability measure on $\mathbb R$ such that $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\widehat\nu\subset(-\epsilon_0\Delta,\epsilon_0\Delta)$. Then, for every finite $p\ge4$ and every complex coefficient sequence, $$\left(\int_\mathbb R \left\|\sum_{i=1}^d a_i e^{i\lambda_i t}\right\|^p \,\,\mathrm d\nu(t)\right)^{1/p} \le \left(\sum_{i=1}^d \|a_i\|^{r_*}\right)^{1/r_*}. \label{eq:src-13}$$ The same estimate holds for coefficients in a finite-dimensional Hilbert space $\mathcal H$ when the exponentials act diagonally: in an orthonormal basis, the $\xi$-th component may use frequencies $\lambda_{i,\xi}$, provided they are $\Delta$-separated as $i$ varies, for each fixed $\xi$. The right side is then $(\sum_i\left\\|a_i\right\\|_{\mathcal H}^{r_*})^{1/r_*}$. All these estimates have constant one.
  - Explanation: A finite Fourier sum of separated frequencies against a band-limited probability measure has an improved coefficient exponent with operator constant exactly one, also for diagonal Hilbert-valued coefficients.
- **claim-quantitative-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-phase-quantile-gap)): For a separated frequency list as above, define $$\mathcal Q=\{(i,j,k,l): \|\lambda_i+\lambda_j-\lambda_k-\lambda_l\| <\epsilon_0\Delta\}.$$ Let $x_i\ge0$, $\sum_i x_i=1$, and put $$Q(x)=\sum_{(i,j,k,l)\in\mathcal Q} (x_i x_j x_k x_l)^{3/4}.$$ Then $Q(x)\le1$. If $0<\kappa<1$ and $\max_i x_i\le1-\kappa$, then $$Q(x)\le1-\delta_\kappa, \qquad \delta_\kappa= \frac{\kappa^4(1-\kappa/2)^2}{4096} \ge 2^{-14}\kappa^4. \label{phase:quantitative-gap}$$
  - Explanation: When no coefficient dominates, the fourth-moment interaction sum has a definite deficit from one, quantified in the dominance parameter.
- **claim-full-root-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-weight-variation)): Fix an eligible path from a root $v_0(T)$ through level $k$. At every spatial point, $$\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}R_i \le K\log\frac{n_{v_0(T)}}{n_{v_k(T)}}, \qquad \sum_{i=0}^{k-1}P_i =\log\frac{n_{v_0(T)}}{n_{v_k(T)}}. \label{hier:full-root-telescope}$$ For a fixed starting level $h\le k_T$, define, for $h\le k\le k_T$, $$\omega_{T,h,k}(\zeta,z) =n_{v_k(T)}(z)^{-\mu/K} \prod_{i=h}^{k-1}w_{v_{i+1}(T)}(\zeta,z).$$ If $0\le\Re\zeta_\sigma\le\mu$ for every $\sigma$, then the supremum plus total variation of this sequence, with the endpoint $k$ varying, is bounded by $$\sup_{h\le k\le k_T}\|\omega_{T,h,k}\| +\sum_{k=h}^{k_T-1} \|\omega_{T,h,k+1}-\omega_{T,h,k}\| \le C h_T^{-\mu/K} \left[1+K(1+\left\\|\zeta\right\\|_2) \log\frac{n_{v_0(T)}}{h_T}\right]. \label{eq:src-16}$$ The same bound holds upon restricting the endpoints to a subinterval.
  - Explanation: Along an eligible path the logarithmic losses telescope across all shifts, so the analytic weights have bounded variation with no cost proportional to depth.
- **claim-amplitude-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-amplitudes)): On $Z_0$, for $0\le\Re\zeta_\sigma\le\mu$, $$\left\\|A_v(\zeta,z)\right\\|_{\mathcal H} \le L^C(1+\left\\|\zeta\right\\|_2)n_v(z). \label{eq:src-17}$$ More precisely, the contribution of any one eligible top $T$ to $A_v$, also when restricted to any interval of packet lengths, is bounded by $$L^C(1+\left\\|\zeta\right\\|_2)\|q_T(z)\|h_T(z)^4. \label{hier:individual-amplitude}$$ If all eligible tops of $v$ are insignificant at $z\in Z_0$, then $$\left\\|A_v(\zeta,z)\right\\|_{\mathcal H} \le L^C(1+\left\\|\zeta\right\\|_2)e^{-L\log L}n_v(z). \label{hier:insignificant-amplitude}$$
  - Explanation: Analytic amplitudes are bounded by a polynomial factor times the mass, individual tops by their weight power, and insignificant tops are exponentially suppressed.
- **claim-mass-motion** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-mass-motion)): Fix $R,J,j$ as above and an interval $I$ of horizontal length $H_j$ on a line of slope $s_R$. Consider the masses $n_J,n_c,m_c$ in <a href="#eq:src-18" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-18">[eq:src-18]</a>, the masses used in the factors $\lambda_c$, and those used in any endpoint multiplier of a packet contributing to $A_c$. Each such mass, and each of its positive subset-complement differences used below, is a sum of weights of tops $T$ satisfying $$\|s_T-s_R\|\le C\rho_j, \qquad d_T\le\rho_j/B^2. \label{hier:motion-locality}$$ There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mov}}=C G^{-1/2}$, every nonzero such mass $a$ satisfies $$\|\partial_x\log a\|\le\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mov}}}{H_j} \quad\text{almost everywhere on }I. \label{hier:mass-log-derivative}$$ If $r=a/(a+b)$ is one of the ratios of a submass to its containing mass, then $$\|\partial_x\log r\| \le\frac{2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mov}}}{H_j}(1-r) \le\frac{2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mov}}}{H_j}(-\log r). \label{hier:ratio-derivative}$$ When $b=0$, the ratio is identically one and its derivative is zero. In particular, nonzero $n_J-n_c$ and $m_c-n_c$ have the relative motion bound <a href="#hier:mass-log-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hier:mass-log-derivative">[hier:mass-log-derivative]</a>. All these masses vary by a factor $1+O(G^{-1/2})$ across $I$.* *For the parameters <a href="#hier:vertex-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hier:vertex-parameters">[hier:vertex-parameters]</a>, the block factors also satisfy, for $x,x_0\in I$, $$\left\|\frac{\lambda_c(x)}{\lambda_c(x_0)}-1\right\| \le G^{-c_0} \label{hier:complex-transition-motion}$$ for some absolute $c_0>0$ and all sufficiently large $L$.
  - Explanation: Masses and mass ratios vary slowly along a mesh cell on a reference line, so block factors are nearly constant on the cell.
- **claim-moving-weights** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-moving-weights)): In the setting of [hier:mass-motion](#hier:mass-motion), fix a top $T$ contributing to $A_c$, and take endpoint weights $\omega_k=\omega_{T,i_{j+1},k}$. If $z_0\in I\cap Z_0$ and the parameters satisfy <a href="#hier:vertex-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hier:vertex-parameters">[hier:vertex-parameters]</a>, then, uniformly for $z'\in I$, $$\begin{aligned} \left\\|(\omega_k(z'))_k\right\\|_{\mathrm{BV}} &\le L^C h_T(z_0)^{-4}, \label{hier:moving-weight-bv}\\ \left\\|(\omega_k(z')-\omega_k(z_0))_k\right\\|_{\mathrm{BV}} &\le G^{-1/2}L^C h_T(z_0)^{-4}. \label{hier:moving-weight-difference} \end{aligned}$$ Both conclusions hold for any interval of endpoints. Neither assumes that $z'$ belongs to $Z_0$.
  - Explanation: Moving endpoint weights have bounded variation controlled from one good snapshot, without assuming the displaced points are good.
- **claim-amplitude-motion** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-amplitude-motion)): Fix a block child $c$ of $J$, a path top $R$ eligible in $J$, and an $H_j$ cell on a line of slope $s_R$. Let $t_c$ be the angular center of $c$. If $z_0\in Z_0$ lies in that cell and $z'=z_0+(b,s_Rb)$ is any other point in the cell, then, for the parameters <a href="#hier:vertex-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hier:vertex-parameters">[hier:vertex-parameters]</a>, $$\left\\|A_c(\zeta,z')-\mathcal D_c(b)A_c(\zeta,z_0)\right\\|_{\mathcal H} \le G^{-c_0}n_c(z_0), \qquad \mathcal D_c(b)_{\nu\nu} =\exp\bigl(i(s_R-t_c)\eta_\nu b\bigr). \label{eq:src-21}$$ Here $c_0>0$ is absolute and may also be used, after decreasing it, in <a href="#hier:complex-transition-motion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hier:complex-transition-motion">[hier:complex-transition-motion]</a>. The estimates hold at all displaced points in the cell, not just its points in $Z_0$.
  - Explanation: A finer amplitude at a displaced point is predicted from one good snapshot by a diagonal phase rotation, up to a small relative error.
- **claim-good-paths** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-good-paths)): There is a fixed exponent $C_{\mathrm{path}}$, chosen independently of the mass cutoff below, such that the following construction is valid. Call $R$ bad at $z$ if any of $\mathcal E_R(z),\mathcal S_R(z),\mathcal V_{R,\sigma}(z)$, $0\le\sigma<K$, exceeds $L^{C_{\mathrm{path}}}$. Otherwise call it good at $z$. For every fixed angular translation, $$\sum_R\int_{\mathbb R^2}h_R(z)\mathbf 1_{\{R\text{ bad at }z\}}\,\,\mathrm dz \le S e^{-10L}. \label{hier:weighted-bad-paths}$$ The constant $10$ may be replaced by any prescribed absolute constant by increasing the fixed exponent. The measurable set $$Z=Z_\vartheta =\left\{z\in Z_0: \sum_{R\text{ bad at }z}h_R(z)\le e^{-L}\right\} \label{hier:good-set}$$ is independent of $\zeta$ and satisfies $$\|Z_0\setminus Z\|\le S e^{-9L} \le L^Ce^{-5L}\|F\|. \label{hier:good-set-budget}$$ Its removal is therefore allowable in the target estimate <a href="#eq:src-7" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-7">[eq:src-7]</a>, using the original adjoint sum’s $L^2$ bound.
  - Explanation: Paths whose error, square function or variation exceeds a fixed polynomial threshold carry exponentially small weight, so the good set loses little area.
- **claim-path-expansion** (construction, [source](#stmt-hier-path-expansion)): Fix $z\in Z$, a root, a shift, and parameters as above. Starting from a node with $n_J>M$, expand $$V_J=\beta_J \left(n_J-\sum_c n_c+\sum_c V_c\right), \label{hier:exact-expansion}$$ and stop a branch on first reaching a node of mass at most $M$. At each expanding node, split $n_J-\sum_c n_c$ into the individual weights of the tops which do not remain eligible at the block endpoint. At a stopped node split $V_J$ proportionally among its eligible top weights. This gives exactly one terminal path for each eligible root top $R$, with a terminal amount $e_R$ satisfying $$0<e_R\le C_Mh_R, \qquad V_{\mathrm{root}} =\sum_R e_R\prod_{J\text{ active on }R\text{'s path}}\beta_J. \label{hier:top-path-expansion}$$ If $n_{\mathrm{root}}>M$, at least one half of this sum comes from tops which are good at $z$. Consequently, $$V_{\mathrm{root}}(z) \le 2C_M\sum_{\substack{R\text{ eligible at root}\\R\text{ good at }z}} h_R(z)\prod_{J\text{ active on }R\text{'s path}}\beta_J(z). \label{hier:good-path-domination}$$ If $n_{\mathrm{root}}\le M$, <a href="#hier:small-node-amount" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hier:small-node-amount">[hier:small-node-amount]</a> applies instead.
  - Explanation: The root amount expands exactly into top paths with terminal amounts bounded by the top weight, and good paths carry at least half of the total.
- **claim-drops-and-marking** (bound, [source](#stmt-hier-marking)): At a point of $Z_0$, the active path drops satisfy $$\sum_{j:\,n_j>M}u_j \le 1+\log^+\frac{n_0}{M}\le C L. \label{hier:drop-budget}$$ On each line of slope $s_R$, mark an $H_j$ cell $I$ when $$\inf_{z\in I}n_j(z)>M, \qquad \sup_{z\in I}h_j^*(z)\ge\frac12. \label{eq:src-23}$$ Every point of a marked cell is active and has $h_j^*\ge c$ for an absolute $c>0$. At an active point in an unmarked cell with $h_j^*\ge1$, one has $M<n_j<M+1$ for all sufficiently large $L$. There is at most one such index on each active path, and its factor is bounded at points of $Z_0$ by $$\beta_{J_j}\le L^{Cp}(1+M)^C. \label{hier:missing-factor}$$ If an index is inactive at a point, the cell containing that point is unmarked. Finally, even in the last possible block, $$H_j\le\frac{l_R}{B\sqrt G}. \label{hier:mesh-within-top}$$
  - Explanation: Active relative drops sum to at most a multiple of L, marked cells carry a definite drop, and an active unmarked index with a large drop occurs at most once per path.
- **claim-marked-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-pred-marked-comparison)): There is an absolute $c_{\rm av}>0$ with the following property, for all sufficiently large $L$. On a fixed line of slope $s_R$, each marked $H_j$-cell $I$ meeting $Z$ admits a nonnegative function $\Gamma_j$ which is constant on the $H_{j-1}$-subcells of $I$, satisfies $\|I\|^{-1}\int_I\Gamma_j\le1$, and obeys $$\beta_J^0(z)\le \exp\bigl(G^{-c_{\rm av}}u_j(z)\bigr)\Gamma_j(z) \qquad (z\in I). \label{eq:src-24}$$ Set $\Gamma_j=1$ on unmarked cells and on cells not meeting $Z$. The functions can be chosen measurably on each fixed line. No choice jointly measurable in the family of parallel lines is needed.
  - Explanation: On marked cells the actual factor is dominated by a step function with average at most one, up to a loss proportional to the relative path drop.
- **claim-fourier-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-pred-fourier-average)): There is a fixed sufficiently small $\epsilon'>0$ such that, with $$Q=G^{3/4}\rho_{j+1},\qquad \delta=\epsilon' Q/w, \label{pred:separation-bandwidth}$$ every positive probability $\nu$ satisfying $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\widehat\nu\subset(-\delta,\delta)$ obeys $\int\widetilde\beta\,\,\mathrm d\nu\le1$.
  - Explanation: The predicted factor has average at most one against every positive probability whose Fourier support is narrow enough.
- **claim-block-square** (bound, [source](#stmt-res-block-square)): At a point $z\in Z$ where $R$ is a good path, $$\left(\sum_j\left\\|B_{J_j}(z)\right\\|^2\right)^{1/2}\le L^{C_b}. \label{res:block-square-bound}$$ Here the sum can be restricted to any subset of the possible blocks. The fixed exponent $C_b$ is independent of $M$.
  - Explanation: Residual block amplitudes are square-summable with a polynomial bound in L at points where the path is good.
- **claim-common-weight-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-res-common-weight-variation)): Uniformly over the segments and their starting indices, $$\sup_{i_j\le k<i_f}\|\omega_j(k)\| +\sum_{k=i_j}^{i_f-2}\|\omega_j(k+1)-\omega_j(k)\| \le L^{C_w}. \label{res:common-weight-BV}$$ The exponent $C_w$ is fixed before $M$ is chosen.
  - Explanation: The common residual coefficients along a unary segment have polynomially bounded sup plus total variation, using the full-root bound.
- **claim-path-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-res-path-product)): With $M$ chosen by <a href="#res:choice-M" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="res:choice-M">[res:choice-M]</a>, the estimate <a href="#eq:src-22" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-22">[eq:src-22]</a> holds for every path top $R$, every shift, and every vertex parameter with $\left\\|\Im\zeta\right\\|_2\le L^2$. Its constants are independent of the tile collection, angular depth, and Hilbert-space dimension.
  - Explanation: The integral of the top weight times the product of path factors is bounded by a polynomial in L and the cutoff times the integral of the weight.
- **claim-vertex-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-assembly-vertices)): For every nonempty root $v$, every shift $\sigma$, and every parameter with $$\Re\zeta=\mu e_\sigma, \qquad \\|\Im\zeta\\|_2\le L^2,$$ one has $$\label{eq:src-29} \big\\|n_v^{\alpha/p-1}A_v(\zeta)\big\\|_{L^p(Z;\mathcal H)} \le L^C S^{1/p}.$$ The constant and exponent are independent of the shift and the grid translation.
  - Explanation: At vertices of the shift simplex the normalized root amplitude has an Lp bound on the good set with a polynomial loss.
- **claim-interpolation-center** (bound, [source](#stmt-assembly-interpolation)): Equation <a href="#eq:src-29" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-29">[eq:src-29]</a> also holds at $$\zeta^{\mathrm c}=(\mu/K,\ldots,\mu/K).$$
  - Explanation: Three-lines interpolation on a damped analytic family transfers the vertex bound to the central equal-coordinate parameter without a factor depending on K.
- **claim-expected-boundary-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-assembly-boundary-loss)): Let $\epsilon=4G^{-1/4}$. For $z\in Z_0$, $$\label{assembly:expected-error} \mathbb E_\vartheta\\|P_q(z)-\mathcal W_\vartheta(z)\\| \le L^C\epsilon n(z).$$ Here the expectation is normalized Lebesgue measure for the common translation modulo $d(0)$; independent translations at different levels are neither assumed nor needed.
  - Explanation: Averaging over one common angular-grid translation makes the expected boundary-weight error small relative to the mass.
- **claim-improved-popular-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-assembly-improved-model)): The popular adjoint sum satisfies Equation <a href="#eq:src-7" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-7">[eq:src-7]</a>; namely, $$\int_F\\|P\\|\lesssim L^{-20}N\|F\|.$$
  - Explanation: The popular adjoint sum has an integral over the input set with a power saving in L relative to N times the measure of the set.
- **claim-three-model-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-assembly-all-model-estimates)): For the packet forms in <a href="#eq:src-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-3">[eq:src-3]</a>, let $u$ be a bounded measurable real slope, let $F,E\subset\mathbb R^2$ be measurable sets of positive finite measure, and suppose $\\|f\\|_{\mathcal H}\le\mathbf 1_F$ and $\\|g\\|_{\mathcal H}\le\mathbf 1_E$ pointwise. The following estimates hold uniformly over every finite subcollection.* 1. *For $m\ge1$, $$\|\Lambda(f,g)\|\lesssim(1+\log(2m))^C\sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|}.$$* 2. *Suppose $L\ge L_0$, with $\tau,m,W$ chosen by <a href="#red:small-time-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:small-time-parameters">[red:small-time-parameters]</a>, and $L/2\le\log\sqrt{\|E\|/\|F\|}\le2L$. If $\left\|u\right\|\le1$, $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(u)\le C_{\rm Lip}$, and $F,E\subset I\times\mathbb R$ for an interval $I$ of length at most $b_0$, then $$\|\Lambda(f,g)\|\lesssim L^{-20}\sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|}.$$* 3. *For the scalar model with $m=1$ and $\|E\|\le\|F\|$, there is an absolute $c>0$ such that $$\|\Lambda(f,g)\|\lesssim \sqrt{\|F\|\|E\|}\left(\frac{\|E\|}{\|F\|}\right)^c.$$
  - Explanation: The basic, improved and reverse-disparity packet estimates hold uniformly over finite subcollections, which completes the reductions to the band estimate and the main theorem.

```json
{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $a_*\\in(0,1/2)$ and $C_*<\\infty$ such that every Lipschitz field $v:\\mathbb R^2\\to S^1$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(v)\\le1$ satisfies $$\\sup_{0<\\epsilon<a_*}\n \\left\\|H_{v,a_*}^{\\epsilon}f\\right\\|_{L^2(\\mathbb R^2)}\n \\le C_*\\left\\|f\\right\\|_{L^2(\\mathbb R^2)}\n \\qquad(f\\in\\mathcal S(\\mathbb R^2)).$$ Its symmetric principal value therefore satisfies $$\\left\\|H_{v,a_*}f\\right\\|_2\\le C_*\\left\\|f\\right\\|_2,\n \\qquad\n \\big|\\{x:|H_{v,a_*}f(x)|>\\lambda\\}\\big|\n \\le C_*^2\\lambda^{-2}\\left\\|f\\right\\|_2^2\n \\quad(\\lambda>0).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $v:\\mathbb R^2\\to S^1$ be Lipschitz and $0<\\delta<1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-red-fixed-time",
        "exact_quote": "For $\\left|t\\right|\\,C_{\\rm Lip}<1$, define $$A_t h(x,y)=h(x-t,y-tu(x,y)).$$ For $1\\le p<\\infty$, $$\\label{red:translate-bound}\n \\left\\|A_t h\\right\\|_p\n   \\le (1-\\left|t\\right|\\,C_{\\rm Lip})^{-1/p}\\left\\|h\\right\\|_p,$$ and $A_t$ is a contraction on $L^\\infty$. Consequently, replacing either symmetric endpoint at scale $r$ by a measurable endpoint between $c r$ and $C r$, with $c,C>0$ fixed and $\\max(1,C)r C_{\\rm Lip}<1/2$, costs a bounded $L^p$ operator, independently of $r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-band-restricted",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed absolute constants in <a href=\"#red:local-operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"red:local-operator\">[red:local-operator]</a>, there is an absolute $C$ such that, for every field $u$ with $\\left|u\\right|\\le1$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(u)\\le C_{\\rm Lip}$, every $0<\\epsilon<a$, every dyadic $w$, and every vertical band cutoff $P_w$, $$\\label{eq:src-1}\n \\left|\\langle H_{u,a}^{\\epsilon}P_w f,g\\rangle\\right|\n \\le C\\bigl(1+\\left|\\log(|F|/|E|)\\right|\\bigr)^{-3}\n       \\sqrt{|F||E|}$$ whenever $F,E\\subset I\\times\\mathbb R$ have positive finite measure, $\\left|f\\right|\\le\\mathbf 1_F$, $\\left|g\\right|\\le\\mathbf 1_E$, and $\\left|I\\right|\\le b_0$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-red-packet-representation",
        "exact_quote": "The diagonal form $$\\sum_j\\langle\\mathcal A_{u,\\mathcal L}\n                         \\widetilde Q_j f_j,g_j\\rangle$$ is a sum, with absolutely summable scalar coefficients, of averages of forms <a href=\"#eq:src-3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-3\">[eq:src-3]</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-red-model-suffices",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the following bounds hold, uniformly for every admissible finite form $\\Lambda_{\\mathcal S}$ in [red:packets](#red:packets), with the fixed packet constants there. The field $u:\\mathbb R^2\\to\\mathbb R$ is bounded and measurable, the sets $F,E\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ are measurable with positive finite measure, and the input and test satisfy $\\left\\|f(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\\le\\mathbf 1_F(z)$, $\\left\\|g(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\\le\\mathbf 1_E(z)$.*\n\n\n1.  *For $m\\ge1$, there is a fixed exponent $C_1$ such that $$\\label{red:model-basic}\n     |\\Lambda_{\\mathcal S}(f,g)|\n         \\le C(1+\\log(2m))^{C_1}\\sqrt{|F||E|}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For $L\\ge L_0$, with $\\tau,m,W$ as in <a href=\"#red:small-time-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"red:small-time-parameters\">[red:small-time-parameters]</a>, assume in addition that $\\left|u\\right|\\le1$, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(u)\\le C_{\\rm Lip}$, and $F,E\\subset I\\times\\mathbb R$ for an interval $I$ with $\\left|I\\right|\\le b_0$. Whenever $L/2\\le\\log\\sqrt{|E|/|F|}\\le2L$, $$\\label{red:model-improved}\n     |\\Lambda_{\\mathcal S}(f,g)|\n           \\le C L^{-20}\\sqrt{|F||E|}.$$*\n\n\n3.  *For $m=1$, one scalar component, and $|E|\\le|F|$, there is a fixed $c>0$ such that $$\\label{red:model-reverse}\n     |\\Lambda_{\\mathcal S}(f,g)|\n           \\le C\\sqrt{|F||E|}(|E|/|F|)^c.$$*\n\n*Then holds. The constants remain uniform in $w$, $\\epsilon$, and the number of retained scales.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tile-prop-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let the bin Hilbert space be $\\mathcal H=\\mathbb C^J$, where $J\\leq C_{\\rm bin}m$. If $\\left\\|g(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\\leq\\mathbf 1_E(z)$ and $D_E(s)\\leq\\gamma$ for every $s\\in\\mathcal S$, with $0<\\gamma\\leq C$, then $$\\label{eq:src-4}\n |\\Lambda_{\\mathcal S}(f,g)|\n \\leq C(1+\\log(2m))^C\\gamma^{1/4}\n             |E|^{1/2}\\left\\|f\\right\\|_{L^2(\\mathcal H)}.$$ In the scalar case, if $|f|\\leq\\mathbf 1_F$, $|g|\\leq\\mathbf 1_E$, and $0<|E|<|F|<\\infty$, then $$\\label{tile:eq-reverse-disparity}\n |\\Lambda_{\\mathcal S}(f,g)|\n \\leq C\\sqrt{|F||E|}\n                \\left(\\frac{|E|}{|F|}\\right)^{9/20}.$$ Both conclusions hold uniformly for every subcollection of $\\mathcal S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be separated tiles satisfying <a href=\"#tile:eq-tree\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"tile:eq-tree\">[tile:eq-tree]</a>, and let $\\Upsilon_s$ be either of their packet families $\\Phi_s$ or $\\Psi_s$. For every fixed $J_0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left\\|\\mathfrak p_{T,J_0}\n                \\sum_{s\\in\\mathcal T}c_s\\Upsilon_s\\right\\|_2\n &\\leq C_{J_0}\n          \\left(\\sum_{s\\in\\mathcal T}|c_s|^2|R_s|\\right)^{1/2},\n                  \\label{tile:eq-synthesis}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\mathfrak p_{T,J_0}\n       \\sup_{a<b}\\left|\n        \\sum_{\\substack{s\\in\\mathcal T\\\\a<\\ell_s\\leq b}}\n                  c_s\\Upsilon_s\\right|\\right\\|_2\n &\\leq C_{J_0}\n          \\left(\\sum_{s\\in\\mathcal T}|c_s|^2|R_s|\\right)^{1/2}.\n                  \\label{tile:eq-maximal-synthesis}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same statements hold for Hilbert-valued sums $\\bigl(\\sum_s c_{s,j}\\Upsilon_{s,j}\\bigr)_j$, with $\\sum_s\\left\\|c_s\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}^2|R_s|$ on the right and constants independent of $J$. Bounded scalar factors depending on $s,j$, such as $b_{s,j}(t)$, can be incorporated into the coefficients.",

      },

      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be separated tiles satisfying <a href=\"#tile:eq-tree\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"tile:eq-tree\">[tile:eq-tree]</a>, and let $\\Upsilon_s$ be either of their packet families $\\Phi_s$ or $\\Psi_s$. For every fixed $J_0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left\\|\\mathfrak p_{T,J_0}\n                \\sum_{s\\in\\mathcal T}c_s\\Upsilon_s\\right\\|_2\n &\\leq C_{J_0}\n          \\left(\\sum_{s\\in\\mathcal T}|c_s|^2|R_s|\\right)^{1/2},\n                  \\label{tile:eq-synthesis}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\mathfrak p_{T,J_0}\n       \\sup_{a<b}\\left|\n        \\sum_{\\substack{s\\in\\mathcal T\\\\a<\\ell_s\\leq b}}\n                  c_s\\Upsilon_s\\right|\\right\\|_2\n &\\leq C_{J_0}\n          \\left(\\sum_{s\\in\\mathcal T}|c_s|^2|R_s|\\right)^{1/2}.\n                  \\label{tile:eq-maximal-synthesis}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same statements hold for Hilbert-valued sums $\\bigl(\\sum_s c_{s,j}\\Upsilon_{s,j}\\bigr)_j$, with $\\sum_s\\left\\|c_s\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}^2|R_s|$ on the right and constants independent of $J$. Bounded scalar factors depending on $s,j$, such as $b_{s,j}(t)$, can be incorporated into the coefficients.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tile-lem-density",
        "exact_quote": "For $0<\\eta\\leq C$, all tiles with $D_E(s)>\\eta$ can be assigned to keeper boxes $R_T$, of dyadic length $l_T\\leq\\tau$, width $W$, and slope $t_T$, with the following properties. There are pairwise disjoint measurable sets $E_T$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n E_T\\subset E\\cap K_\\eta R_T\n                 \\cap\\{u\\in\\Omega(t_T,l_T)\\},\\qquad\n |E_T|\\geq c\\eta|R_T|,\n                  \\label{tile:eq-density-witnesses}\\\\\n \\sum_T|R_T|\\leq C\\eta^{-1}|E|,\\qquad\n K_\\eta\\leq C(1+\\eta^{-1/100}),\n                  \\label{tile:eq-keeper-budget}\\\\\n \\ell_s\\leq l_T,\\qquad t_T\\in\\Omega_s,\\qquad\n z_s\\in C K_\\eta R_T\n \\quad\\text{for each tile assigned to }T.\n                  \\label{tile:eq-density-assignment}\n\\end{gathered}$$ After partitioning by centers, the assigned tiles form ordinary test trees of total top area at most $$\\label{tile:eq-density-budget}\n C\\eta^{-1}(1+\\eta^{-1/20})|E|.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-forest-lem-mask",
        "exact_quote": "The mask has Fourier support $$\\label{forest:eq-mask-spectrum}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\widehat q_T\\subset\n \\left\\{(\\xi,\\eta):\n       |\\xi+s_T\\eta|\\le\\frac{r_L}{L^A l_T},\\quad\n       |\\eta|\\le\\frac{r_L}{L^A W}\\right\\}.$$ For sufficiently large $L$, with all other exponents fixed, $$\\begin{aligned}\n |q_T(z)|&\\le \\exp(-L\\log L)\n       &&\\text{if }\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_T(z)>K_0,\n       \\label{forest:eq-mask-outside}\\\\\n |q_T(z)|h_T(z)^{-1}&\\le C\n       &&\\text{for all }z,\n       \\label{forest:eq-mask-weight}\\\\\n |1-q_T(z)|&\\le C L^{1-A}\n       &&\\text{if }\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_T(z)\\le L^{A/2}.\n       \\label{forest:eq-mask-inside}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular multiplication by $q_T$ adds at most $O(L^{1-A})$ times the bandwidths of the top box to a Fourier support.",

        "anchor": "stmt-conc-john-nirenberg",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I$ be a finite interval and let $f$ be a locally integrable scalar function on $I$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-conc-top-good",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $J_*>0$ there are a fixed exponent $C_{\\mathrm{top}}$ and a measurable set $\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}$ with $\\left|\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}\\right|\\leq S e^{-J_*L}$ such that, for all tops $T$ and all $z\\notin\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}$, $$\\label{eq:src-11}\n \\sup_{\\mathcal J}\\ \\sup_{\\left|v\\right|\\leq W}\n \\left(\\left\\|F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z+(0,v))\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n       +\\left\\|\\mathcal D F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z+(0,v))\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\\right)\n \\leq L^{C_{\\mathrm{top}}}\\big(h_T^{(1)}(z)\\big)^8.$$ Independently of this exceptional set, for every $z$, every $l_*>0$, and every interval $\\mathcal J\\subset[l_*,\\infty)$, $$\\label{conc:top-horizontal-derivative}\n \\left\\|(\\partial_x+s_T\\partial_y)F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n \\leq \\frac{L^{C_{\\mathrm{top}}}}{l_*}\\big(h_T^{(1)}(z)\\big)^8.$$ All packet orders, $K_1$, and $C_{\\mathrm{top}}$ can be fixed before $A$; they are independent of the later mass cutoff and hierarchy depth.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-conc-top-good",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $J_*>0$ there are a fixed exponent $C_{\\mathrm{top}}$ and a measurable set $\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}$ with $\\left|\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}\\right|\\leq S e^{-J_*L}$ such that, for all tops $T$ and all $z\\notin\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}$, $$\\label{eq:src-11}\n \\sup_{\\mathcal J}\\ \\sup_{\\left|v\\right|\\leq W}\n \\left(\\left\\|F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z+(0,v))\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n       +\\left\\|\\mathcal D F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z+(0,v))\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\\right)\n \\leq L^{C_{\\mathrm{top}}}\\big(h_T^{(1)}(z)\\big)^8.$$ Independently of this exceptional set, for every $z$, every $l_*>0$, and every interval $\\mathcal J\\subset[l_*,\\infty)$, $$\\label{conc:top-horizontal-derivative}\n \\left\\|(\\partial_x+s_T\\partial_y)F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n \\leq \\frac{L^{C_{\\mathrm{top}}}}{l_*}\\big(h_T^{(1)}(z)\\big)^8.$$ All packet orders, $K_1$, and $C_{\\mathrm{top}}$ can be fixed before $A$; they are independent of the later mass cutoff and hierarchy depth.",
          "source_id": "PM-083.1"

      "exact_statement": "For every fixed $J_*>0$ there are a fixed exponent $C_{\\mathrm{top}}$ and a measurable set $\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}$ with $\\left|\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}\\right|\\leq S e^{-J_*L}$ such that, for all tops $T$ and all $z\\notin\\mathcal X_{\\rm top}$, $$\\label{eq:src-11}\n \\sup_{\\mathcal J}\\ \\sup_{\\left|v\\right|\\leq W}\n \\left(\\left\\|F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z+(0,v))\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n       +\\left\\|\\mathcal D F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z+(0,v))\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\\right)\n \\leq L^{C_{\\mathrm{top}}}\\big(h_T^{(1)}(z)\\big)^8.$$ Independently of this exceptional set, for every $z$, every $l_*>0$, and every interval $\\mathcal J\\subset[l_*,\\infty)$, $$\\label{conc:top-horizontal-derivative}\n \\left\\|(\\partial_x+s_T\\partial_y)F_{T,\\mathcal J}(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n \\leq \\frac{L^{C_{\\mathrm{top}}}}{l_*}\\big(h_T^{(1)}(z)\\big)^8.$$ All packet orders, $K_1$, and $C_{\\mathrm{top}}$ can be fixed before $A$; they are independent of the later mass cutoff and hierarchy depth.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-conc-mask-removal",
        "exact_quote": "Given any fixed $H>0$, choose $A$ sufficiently large after $K_1$ and $C_{\\mathrm{top}}$, and then choose a sufficiently large fixed count threshold exponent $C_n$. There is a measurable set $F_{\\rm conc}\\subset F$ such that $$\\left|F\\setminus F_{\\rm conc}\\right|\\leq L^{-H}\\left|F\\right|,\n \\qquad\n \\int_{F_{\\rm conc}}\\left\\|P-P_q\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n       \\leq L^{-H}N\\left|F\\right|,\n \\qquad P_q=\\sum_sq_{T(s)}\\phi_s,$$ and <a href=\"#eq:src-11\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-11\">[eq:src-11]</a> holds there for every top. On this set, $$\\label{eq:src-12}\n n(z)=\\sum_T h_T(z)\\leq NL^{C_n}.$$ One has $K_0=L^{A+2}>K_1$, so both <a href=\"#eq:src-11\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-11\">[eq:src-11]</a> and <a href=\"#conc:top-horizontal-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conc:top-horizontal-derivative\">[conc:top-horizontal-derivative]</a> also hold with $h_T$ in place of $h_T^{(1)}$.",

On this set, $$\\label{eq:src-12}\n n(z)=\\sum_T h_T(z)\\leq NL^{C_n}.$$ One has $K_0=L^{A+2}>K_1$, so both <a href=\"#eq:src-11\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-11\">[eq:src-11]</a> and <a href=\"#conc:top-horizontal-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conc:top-horizontal-derivative\">[conc:top-horizontal-derivative]</a> also hold with $h_T$ in place of $h_T^{(1)}$.",

On this set, $$\\label{eq:src-12}\n n(z)=\\sum_T h_T(z)\\leq NL^{C_n}.$$ One has $K_0=L^{A+2}>K_1$, so both <a href=\"#eq:src-11\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-11\">[eq:src-11]</a> and <a href=\"#conc:top-horizontal-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conc:top-horizontal-derivative\">[conc:top-horizontal-derivative]</a> also hold with $h_T$ in place of $h_T^{(1)}$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-count-simultaneous",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $C_D$ with the following property. For every fixed $J>0$ and all sufficiently large $L$, there is a measurable set $\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{count}}\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ such that $$\\label{count:exceptional-budget}\n |\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{count}}|\\leq S e^{-JL}.$$ Define $$\\label{count:good-set}\n Z_0=F_{\\mathrm{conc}}\\setminus\\mathcal E_{\\mathrm{count}},\n \\qquad D=L^{C_D}.$$ For every $z\\in Z_0$, every $\\rho>0$, and every list $\\mathcal L$ of tops significant at $z$ satisfying $$\\label{count:list-hypotheses}\n \\begin{split}\n &\\max_{T\\in\\mathcal L}s_T-\\min_{T\\in\\mathcal L}s_T\\leq B\\rho,\n \\qquad d_T\\leq\\rho/B^2\\quad(T\\in\\mathcal L),\\\\\n &|s_T-s_{T'}|\\geq\\rho\\quad(T,T'\\in\\mathcal L,\\ T\\ne T'),\n \\end{split}$$ one has $\\#\\mathcal L\\leq D/100$. The exponent $C_D$ depends only on the fixed geometric constants, including the already chosen mask exponent $A$. It is independent of the number of tops, the number of scales, and the bin Hilbert space.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-count-line-lemma",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(f_b)_{b\\in\\mathcal B}$ be a countable family of measurable functions on $\\mathbb R$ such that $0\\leq f_b\\leq1$ and $\\sum_b f_b\\leq C_0$, where $C_0\\geq1$. Fix $H>0$ and $0<\\vartheta\\leq1$. There is a measurable function $\\mathcal N:\\mathbb R\\to[0,\\infty]$ with the following properties. If $\\mathcal B_x$ is any collection of distinct indices such that, for every $b\\in\\mathcal B_x$, some interval $I_b$ containing $x$ satisfies $$\\label{count:line-average}\n 0<|I_b|\\leq H,\\qquad |I_b|^{-1}\\int_{I_b}f_b\\geq\\vartheta,$$ then $\\#\\mathcal B_x\\leq\\mathcal N(x)$. Moreover, for every bounded interval $U$ and every $\\lambda\\geq0$, $$\\label{count:line-tail}\n |\\{x\\in U:\\mathcal N(x)>\\lambda\\}|\n \\leq C(|U|+H)\\exp(-c\\vartheta\\lambda/C_0),$$ where $C,c>0$ are absolute. If the $f_b$ depend jointly measurably on an additional parameter, $\\mathcal N$ can be chosen jointly measurable in that parameter and $x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-count-line-lemma",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(f_b)_{b\\in\\mathcal B}$ be a countable family of measurable functions on $\\mathbb R$ such that $0\\leq f_b\\leq1$ and $\\sum_b f_b\\leq C_0$, where $C_0\\geq1$. Fix $H>0$ and $0<\\vartheta\\leq1$. There is a measurable function $\\mathcal N:\\mathbb R\\to[0,\\infty]$ with the following properties. If $\\mathcal B_x$ is any collection of distinct indices such that, for every $b\\in\\mathcal B_x$, some interval $I_b$ containing $x$ satisfies $$\\label{count:line-average}\n 0<|I_b|\\leq H,\\qquad |I_b|^{-1}\\int_{I_b}f_b\\geq\\vartheta,$$ then $\\#\\mathcal B_x\\leq\\mathcal N(x)$. Moreover, for every bounded interval $U$ and every $\\lambda\\geq0$, $$\\label{count:line-tail}\n |\\{x\\in U:\\mathcal N(x)>\\lambda\\}|\n \\leq C(|U|+H)\\exp(-c\\vartheta\\lambda/C_0),$$ where $C,c>0$ are absolute. If the $f_b$ depend jointly measurably on an additional parameter, $\\mathcal N$ can be chosen jointly measurable in that parameter and $x$.",
          "source_id": "PM-083.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(f_b)_{b\\in\\mathcal B}$ be a countable family of measurable functions on $\\mathbb R$ such that $0\\leq f_b\\leq1$ and $\\sum_b f_b\\leq C_0$, where $C_0\\geq1$. Fix $H>0$ and $0<\\vartheta\\leq1$. There is a measurable function $\\mathcal N:\\mathbb R\\to[0,\\infty]$ with the following properties. If $\\mathcal B_x$ is any collection of distinct indices such that, for every $b\\in\\mathcal B_x$, some interval $I_b$ containing $x$ satisfies $$\\label{count:line-average}\n 0<|I_b|\\leq H,\\qquad |I_b|^{-1}\\int_{I_b}f_b\\geq\\vartheta,$$ then $\\#\\mathcal B_x\\leq\\mathcal N(x)$. Moreover, for every bounded interval $U$ and every $\\lambda\\geq0$, $$\\label{count:line-tail}\n |\\{x\\in U:\\mathcal N(x)>\\lambda\\}|\n \\leq C(|U|+H)\\exp(-c\\vartheta\\lambda/C_0),$$ where $C,c>0$ are absolute. If the $f_b$ depend jointly measurably on an additional parameter, $\\mathcal N$ can be chosen jointly measurable in that parameter and $x$.",
      "explanation": "A bounded family of functions with bounded sum has an exponentially distributed count of indices having a large average on some interval through a point.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-count-line-lemma",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(f_b)_{b\\in\\mathcal B}$ be a countable family of measurable functions on $\\mathbb R$",
          "source_id": "PM-083.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-phase-finite-fourier",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_d\\in\\mathbb R$, where $d\\le D$, satisfy $\\left|\\lambda_i-\\lambda_j\\right|\\ge\\Delta>0$ for $i\\ne j$. Let $\\nu$ be any positive probability measure on $\\mathbb R$ such that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\widehat\\nu\\subset(-\\epsilon_0\\Delta,\\epsilon_0\\Delta)$. Then, for every finite $p\\ge4$ and every complex coefficient sequence, $$\\left(\\int_\\mathbb R\n       \\left|\\sum_{i=1}^d a_i e^{i\\lambda_i t}\\right|^p\n       \\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(t)\\right)^{1/p}\n \\le \\left(\\sum_{i=1}^d |a_i|^{r_*}\\right)^{1/r_*}.\n \\label{eq:src-13}$$ The same estimate holds for coefficients in a finite-dimensional Hilbert space $\\mathcal H$ when the exponentials act diagonally: in an orthonormal basis, the $\\xi$-th component may use frequencies $\\lambda_{i,\\xi}$, provided they are $\\Delta$-separated as $i$ varies, for each fixed $\\xi$. The right side is then $(\\sum_i\\left\\|a_i\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}^{r_*})^{1/r_*}$. All these estimates have constant one.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-phase-quantile-gap",
        "exact_quote": "For a separated frequency list as above, define $$\\mathcal Q=\\{(i,j,k,l):\n    |\\lambda_i+\\lambda_j-\\lambda_k-\\lambda_l|\n       <\\epsilon_0\\Delta\\}.$$ Let $x_i\\ge0$, $\\sum_i x_i=1$, and put $$Q(x)=\\sum_{(i,j,k,l)\\in\\mathcal Q}\n                (x_i x_j x_k x_l)^{3/4}.$$ Then $Q(x)\\le1$. If $0<\\kappa<1$ and $\\max_i x_i\\le1-\\kappa$, then $$Q(x)\\le1-\\delta_\\kappa,\n \\qquad\n \\delta_\\kappa=\n       \\frac{\\kappa^4(1-\\kappa/2)^2}{4096}\n       \\ge 2^{-14}\\kappa^4.\n \\label{phase:quantitative-gap}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-phase-quantile-gap",

        "anchor": "stmt-hier-weight-variation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an eligible path from a root $v_0(T)$ through level $k$. At every spatial point, $$\\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}R_i\n \\le K\\log\\frac{n_{v_0(T)}}{n_{v_k(T)}},\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}P_i\n =\\log\\frac{n_{v_0(T)}}{n_{v_k(T)}}.\n \\label{hier:full-root-telescope}$$ For a fixed starting level $h\\le k_T$, define, for $h\\le k\\le k_T$, $$\\omega_{T,h,k}(\\zeta,z)\n =n_{v_k(T)}(z)^{-\\mu/K}\n   \\prod_{i=h}^{k-1}w_{v_{i+1}(T)}(\\zeta,z).$$ If $0\\le\\Re\\zeta_\\sigma\\le\\mu$ for every $\\sigma$, then the supremum plus total variation of this sequence, with the endpoint $k$ varying, is bounded by $$\\sup_{h\\le k\\le k_T}|\\omega_{T,h,k}|\n +\\sum_{k=h}^{k_T-1}\n       |\\omega_{T,h,k+1}-\\omega_{T,h,k}|\n \\le C h_T^{-\\mu/K}\n \\left[1+K(1+\\left\\|\\zeta\\right\\|_2)\n                   \\log\\frac{n_{v_0(T)}}{h_T}\\right].\n \\label{eq:src-16}$$ The same bound holds upon restricting the endpoints to a subinterval.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hier-amplitudes",
        "exact_quote": "On $Z_0$, for $0\\le\\Re\\zeta_\\sigma\\le\\mu$, $$\\left\\|A_v(\\zeta,z)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n \\le L^C(1+\\left\\|\\zeta\\right\\|_2)n_v(z).\n \\label{eq:src-17}$$ More precisely, the contribution of any one eligible top $T$ to $A_v$, also when restricted to any interval of packet lengths, is bounded by $$L^C(1+\\left\\|\\zeta\\right\\|_2)|q_T(z)|h_T(z)^4.\n \\label{hier:individual-amplitude}$$ If all eligible tops of $v$ are insignificant at $z\\in Z_0$, then $$\\left\\|A_v(\\zeta,z)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n \\le L^C(1+\\left\\|\\zeta\\right\\|_2)e^{-L\\log L}n_v(z).\n \\label{hier:insignificant-amplitude}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hier-mass-motion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $R,J,j$ as above and an interval $I$ of horizontal length $H_j$ on a line of slope $s_R$. Consider the masses $n_J,n_c,m_c$ in <a href=\"#eq:src-18\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-18\">[eq:src-18]</a>, the masses used in the factors $\\lambda_c$, and those used in any endpoint multiplier of a packet contributing to $A_c$. Each such mass, and each of its positive subset-complement differences used below, is a sum of weights of tops $T$ satisfying $$|s_T-s_R|\\le C\\rho_j,\n \\qquad d_T\\le\\rho_j/B^2.\n \\label{hier:motion-locality}$$ There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, with $\\varepsilon_{\\mathrm{mov}}=C G^{-1/2}$, every nonzero such mass $a$ satisfies $$|\\partial_x\\log a|\\le\\frac{\\varepsilon_{\\mathrm{mov}}}{H_j}\n \\quad\\text{almost everywhere on }I.\n \\label{hier:mass-log-derivative}$$ If $r=a/(a+b)$ is one of the ratios of a submass to its containing mass, then $$|\\partial_x\\log r|\n \\le\\frac{2\\varepsilon_{\\mathrm{mov}}}{H_j}(1-r)\n \\le\\frac{2\\varepsilon_{\\mathrm{mov}}}{H_j}(-\\log r).\n \\label{hier:ratio-derivative}$$ When $b=0$, the ratio is identically one and its derivative is zero. In particular, nonzero $n_J-n_c$ and $m_c-n_c$ have the relative motion bound <a href=\"#hier:mass-log-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hier:mass-log-derivative\">[hier:mass-log-derivative]</a>. All these masses vary by a factor $1+O(G^{-1/2})$ across $I$.*\n\n\n*For the parameters <a href=\"#hier:vertex-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hier:vertex-parameters\">[hier:vertex-parameters]</a>, the block factors also satisfy, for $x,x_0\\in I$, $$\\left|\\frac{\\lambda_c(x)}{\\lambda_c(x_0)}-1\\right|\n \\le G^{-c_0}\n \\label{hier:complex-transition-motion}$$ for some absolute $c_0>0$ and all sufficiently large $L$.",

In particular, nonzero $n_J-n_c$ and $m_c-n_c$ have the relative motion bound <a href=\"#hier:mass-log-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hier:mass-log-derivative\">[hier:mass-log-derivative]</a>. All these masses vary by a factor $1+O(G^{-1/2})$ across $I$.*\n\n\n*For the parameters <a href=\"#hier:vertex-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hier:vertex-parameters\">[hier:vertex-parameters]</a>, the block factors also satisfy, for $x,x_0\\in I$, $$\\left|\\frac{\\lambda_c(x)}{\\lambda_c(x_0)}-1\\right|\n \\le G^{-c_0}\n \\label{hier:complex-transition-motion}$$ for some absolute $c_0>0$ and all sufficiently large $L$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-hier-amplitude-motion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a block child $c$ of $J$, a path top $R$ eligible in $J$, and an $H_j$ cell on a line of slope $s_R$. Let $t_c$ be the angular center of $c$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hier-good-paths",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $C_{\\mathrm{path}}$, chosen independently of the mass cutoff below, such that the following construction is valid. Call $R$ bad at $z$ if any of $\\mathcal E_R(z),\\mathcal S_R(z),\\mathcal V_{R,\\sigma}(z)$, $0\\le\\sigma<K$, exceeds $L^{C_{\\mathrm{path}}}$. Otherwise call it good at $z$. For every fixed angular translation, $$\\sum_R\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}h_R(z)\\mathbf 1_{\\{R\\text{ bad at }z\\}}\\,\\,\\mathrm dz\n \\le S e^{-10L}.\n \\label{hier:weighted-bad-paths}$$ The constant $10$ may be replaced by any prescribed absolute constant by increasing the fixed exponent. The measurable set $$Z=Z_\\vartheta\n =\\left\\{z\\in Z_0:\n       \\sum_{R\\text{ bad at }z}h_R(z)\\le e^{-L}\\right\\}\n \\label{hier:good-set}$$ is independent of $\\zeta$ and satisfies $$|Z_0\\setminus Z|\\le S e^{-9L}\n \\le L^Ce^{-5L}|F|.\n \\label{hier:good-set-budget}$$ Its removal is therefore allowable in the target estimate <a href=\"#eq:src-7\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-7\">[eq:src-7]</a>, using the original adjoint sum’s $L^2$ bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-hier-good-paths",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $C_{\\mathrm{path}}$, chosen independently of the mass cutoff below, such that the following construction is valid.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hier-path-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $z\\in Z$, a root, a shift, and parameters as above. Starting from a node with $n_J>M$, expand $$V_J=\\beta_J\n      \\left(n_J-\\sum_c n_c+\\sum_c V_c\\right),\n \\label{hier:exact-expansion}$$ and stop a branch on first reaching a node of mass at most $M$. At each expanding node, split $n_J-\\sum_c n_c$ into the individual weights of the tops which do not remain eligible at the block endpoint. At a stopped node split $V_J$ proportionally among its eligible top weights. This gives exactly one terminal path for each eligible root top $R$, with a terminal amount $e_R$ satisfying $$0<e_R\\le C_Mh_R,\n \\qquad\n V_{\\mathrm{root}}\n =\\sum_R e_R\\prod_{J\\text{ active on }R\\text{'s path}}\\beta_J.\n \\label{hier:top-path-expansion}$$ If $n_{\\mathrm{root}}>M$, at least one half of this sum comes from tops which are good at $z$. Consequently, $$V_{\\mathrm{root}}(z)\n \\le 2C_M\\sum_{\\substack{R\\text{ eligible at root}\\\\R\\text{ good at }z}}\n     h_R(z)\\prod_{J\\text{ active on }R\\text{'s path}}\\beta_J(z).\n \\label{hier:good-path-domination}$$ If $n_{\\mathrm{root}}\\le M$, <a href=\"#hier:small-node-amount\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hier:small-node-amount\">[hier:small-node-amount]</a> applies instead.",
        "source_id": "PM-083.1"
      },

At each expanding node, split $n_J-\\sum_c n_c$ into the individual weights of the tops which do not remain eligible at the block endpoint. At a stopped node split $V_J$ proportionally among its eligible top weights. This gives exactly one terminal path for each eligible root top $R$, with a terminal amount $e_R$ satisfying $$0<e_R\\le C_Mh_R,\n \\qquad\n V_{\\mathrm{root}}\n =\\sum_R e_R\\prod_{J\\text{ active on }R\\text{'s path}}\\beta_J.\n \\label{hier:top-path-expansion}$$ If $n_{\\mathrm{root}}>M$, at least one half of this sum comes from tops which are good at $z$. Consequently, $$V_{\\mathrm{root}}(z)\n \\le 2C_M\\sum_{\\substack{R\\text{ eligible at root}\\\\R\\text{ good at }z}}\n     h_R(z)\\prod_{J\\text{ active on }R\\text{'s path}}\\beta_J(z).\n \\label{hier:good-path-domination}$$ If $n_{\\mathrm{root}}\\le M$, <a href=\"#hier:small-node-amount\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hier:small-node-amount\">[hier:small-node-amount]</a> applies instead.",

Consequently, $$V_{\\mathrm{root}}(z)\n \\le 2C_M\\sum_{\\substack{R\\text{ eligible at root}\\\\R\\text{ good at }z}}\n     h_R(z)\\prod_{J\\text{ active on }R\\text{'s path}}\\beta_J(z).\n \\label{hier:good-path-domination}$$ If $n_{\\mathrm{root}}\\le M$, <a href=\"#hier:small-node-amount\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hier:small-node-amount\">[hier:small-node-amount]</a> applies instead.",

        "anchor": "stmt-hier-marking",
        "exact_quote": "At a point of $Z_0$, the active path drops satisfy $$\\sum_{j:\\,n_j>M}u_j\n \\le 1+\\log^+\\frac{n_0}{M}\\le C L.\n \\label{hier:drop-budget}$$ On each line of slope $s_R$, mark an $H_j$ cell $I$ when $$\\inf_{z\\in I}n_j(z)>M,\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{z\\in I}h_j^*(z)\\ge\\frac12.\n \\label{eq:src-23}$$ Every point of a marked cell is active and has $h_j^*\\ge c$ for an absolute $c>0$. At an active point in an unmarked cell with $h_j^*\\ge1$, one has $M<n_j<M+1$ for all sufficiently large $L$. There is at most one such index on each active path, and its factor is bounded at points of $Z_0$ by $$\\beta_{J_j}\\le L^{Cp}(1+M)^C.\n \\label{hier:missing-factor}$$ If an index is inactive at a point, the cell containing that point is unmarked. Finally, even in the last possible block, $$H_j\\le\\frac{l_R}{B\\sqrt G}.\n \\label{hier:mesh-within-top}$$",
```
